### plan-r7.md

# Plan: bound every UCI protocol exchange by one wall-clock deadline (f-20260911-03)

## Goal

MANDATE (fixed, from `f-20260911-03`): an engine that keeps emitting output after `stop` — or
before `readyok` / `uciok` — must not keep the exchange alive past its deadline. Today
`EngineRuntime::stop_current` wraps each `read_line()` in `timeout(self.deadlines.stop, …)`
inside the drain loop, so every `info` line restarts the 5 s budget; `stop_current` never fails,
never poisons, and `terminate_all` can hit `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` with the child unreaped. `wait_for`
(and its twin `wait_for_cancellable`) reset per line the same way.

Acceptance:

* A-1 `stop_current` against an engine that emits `info` indefinitely and never `bestmove`
  returns `Error::EngineTimeout(_)` no later than `deadlines.stop` after it was entered — the
  `stop` write included — and leaves the runtime in `EngineState::Terminating`. The label is
  `"waiting for bestmove after stop"` when the budget expires during the drain and
  `"writing engine command"` when it expires during the `stop` write.
* A-2 Through the actor, that failure reaches `recover_failed_protocol`: the child is terminated
  (`UciIo::terminate` called) and the actor refuses further searches — the existing contract of
  `start_search_stop_timeout_reaps_the_actor_instead_of_accepting_another_go`, now also for the
  chatty engine.
* A-3 `ensure_ready` against an engine that emits non-`readyok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than `deadlines.readyok` after entry, the
  `isready` write included.
* A-4 `init_uci_cancellable` against an engine that emits non-`uciok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for uciok")` no later than `deadlines.uciok` after entry, the `uci`
  write included; against an engine that answers `uciok` and then emits non-`readyok` lines
  indefinitely it returns `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than
  `deadlines.readyok` after the second exchange's entry, the `isready` write included.
  Cancellation behaviour is unchanged: an observed cancellation still returns
  `Error::EngineDisconnected`, through the same (unbiased) `select!` as today — this plan makes
  no new precedence claim.
* A-5 A well-behaved engine is unaffected: every existing engine test passes unchanged, including
  the exact timeout messages asserted at `process.rs` (`"waiting for bestmove after stop"`).
* A-6 Each **chatty-exchange** test (A-1 drain, A-3, both A-4 exchanges) goes red if the
  read-until helper is reverted to a per-line `timeout` (it then never completes and the test's
  outer bound fails it), if the deadline is started after the command write, if the budget is
  scaled beyond its configured value, or if it times out early (each asserts elapsed time from
  exchange entry against both an upper and a lower bound, with its command write delayed).
  Reuse of an earlier exchange's deadline is claimed only for A-4's second exchange, the one
  test whose prior exchange first consumes part of its budget. The write-exhaustion and cancellation tests prove
  their own named claims only.

## Approach

### O-1 Semantics of the deadlines (decides the finding's open question)

`deadlines.stop`, `deadlines.readyok` and `deadlines.uciok` are **wall-clock budgets for one
protocol exchange**, measured from entry into the waiting routine (for `stop_current`: before
`stop` is written), not per-line stall detectors. Reasoning: UCI requires the engine to answer
`stop` with `bestmove` "as soon as possible" and `isready` with `readyok` "as soon as possible";
neither exchange has a legitimate reason to run indefinitely while output keeps arriving. A
slow-but-alive engine that cannot answer `stop` within 5 s is already treated as broken by the
existing silent-timeout path; talking does not make it less broken. The per-line stall semantic
stays exactly where the protocol legitimately runs indefinitely: `deadlines.search` in
`service_search_read` (an infinite `go` streams `info` forever by design) — unchanged.

### O-2 One bounded read-until helper on `EngineRuntime` (rule 11: the three loops are one concept)

`stop_current`'s drain loop, `wait_for` and `wait_for_cancellable` are three copies of "read
lines until a terminal line, fail with `EngineTimeout(<label>)` on budget, `EngineDisconnected`
on EOF". An **exchange** is one command write plus the drain to its terminal line; its deadline
covers both. Replace the three loops with one private helper on `EngineRuntime` that:

* receives one absolute deadline (`tokio::time::Instant`) computed by the caller **once, at
  exchange entry, before the command is written**, and never recomputes it;
* bounds each `read_line()` with `tokio::time::timeout_at(deadline, …)`, so the remaining budget
  shrinks with every line;
* takes the terminal-line predicate (exact trimmed token for `uciok`/`readyok`, parsed
  `UciMessage::BestMove` for stop), the timeout label, and an optional `CancellationToken`
  (cancellation keeps its current precedence and error: `EngineDisconnected`);
* keeps `read_line()`'s existing per-line behaviour (logging, `MAX_ENGINE_LINE_BYTES`).

The command write of an exchange is bounded by the **same** deadline: `send` gains a
deadline-taking form (`timeout_at(deadline, io.write_line(…))`, label unchanged
`"writing engine command"`), and the existing `send(command)` becomes that form with
`now + deadlines.readyok`, so non-exchange writes keep today's bound and there is one write path.
Callers:

* `stop_current`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.stop`, computed before the state transition and
  before `stop` is written; the `stop` write and the drain share it. It keeps its state
  transitions (`Searching → Stopping`, then `Idle` on success / `Terminating` on any error,
  write timeout included). The `Stopping { .. }` re-entry branch (no second `stop` written) gets
  a fresh full budget from entry; see Decisions.
* `ensure_ready`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.readyok`, shared by the `isready` write and the
  drain.
* `init_uci_cancellable`: two exchanges — `uci`/`uciok` with entry + `deadlines.uciok`, then
  `isready`/`readyok` with a fresh entry + `deadlines.readyok` computed when that second
  exchange starts.

`wait_for` / `wait_for_cancellable` either become thin callers of the helper or disappear in
favour of it.

Error strings stay byte-identical: `"waiting for bestmove after stop"`, `"waiting for uciok"`,
`"waiting for readyok"`.

### O-3 Document the invariant where it is written

* `EngineDeadlines` doc comment (`src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs`): state that inside
  `EngineRuntime` `uciok`, `readyok` and `stop` are wall-clock budgets per exchange (command
  write included) and `search` is a per-line stall bound; and that `collect_engine_configuration`
  separately applies `uciok` as a caller-side wall clock over the configuration drain only
  (its `uci` write keeps `send`'s own bound).
* `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md`: one DO bullet — bound every wait for a terminal UCI
  line (`bestmove` after `stop`, `readyok`, `uciok`) by one absolute deadline that output cannot
  reset, never by a per-line timeout; inside `EngineRuntime` (`stop_current`, `ensure_ready`,
  `init_uci_cancellable`) that deadline is computed **before the command is written** and shared
  by the write and every read, because termination can wait behind a whole stop or readiness
  exchange (the actor serializes commands; `stop_generation` holds the lifecycle lease), so a
  second, separate write budget pushes shutdown past `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET`. Only the reads of
  `init_uci_cancellable` observe `interrupt`; its writes are bounded by the same deadline. Name `f-20260911-03` as the incident. (The configuration
  probe, a caller-side wall clock over separate `ConfigureNext` requests, satisfies the first
  clause and is outside the second.)
* `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` is not edited: its comment in `collect_engine_configuration`
  ("the per-line timeout in `next_configuration_line` only protects a silent process") stays
  accurate, because `next_configuration_line_cancellable` is unchanged.

### O-4 Tests (proof of A-1…A-6)

In `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` `mod tests`:

* A test `UciIo` that returns an `info depth 1 …` line after a short sleep on every read,
  forever, never EOF (a small dedicated struct beside `GameCleanupTestIo`/`FakeIo`, or an
  "endless line" option on `FakeIo` — executor's choice; `FakeIo`'s queue is finite and its
  `read_delay` is one-shot, so it cannot express this today). Each read's sleep must be well
  below the budget so a per-line timeout would never fire.
* Runtime-level tests for A-1 (state is `Terminating` afterwards), A-3, A-4 (timeout; plus the
  existing cancellation path still returning `EngineDisconnected` when the token is cancelled
  while the engine is silent), each wrapped in an outer `tokio::time::timeout` several times
  the budget so a per-line regression fails instead of hanging.
* **Deadline precision (A-6):** these tests run under `#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]`, so
  tokio's clock advances deterministically, and assert `elapsed` measured with
  `tokio::time::Instant` from just before the call: `elapsed <= budget` (paused time makes this
  exact; allow at most one read interval of slack only if the helper's structure requires it,
  and state why). Each chatty-exchange test also asserts a **lower bound**
  `elapsed >= budget - read_interval`, so a deadline that fires early (e.g. a reused earlier
  deadline) fails. **Every exchange test delays its own command write** by a substantial
  fraction of that exchange's budget (e.g. 60 %) and still asserts both bounds from entry — the
  case a deadline started after the write fails: `stop` for A-1, `isready` for A-3, `uci` for
  A-4's first exchange. The test IO therefore needs a per-command write delay (keyed by the
  command text), not only a read delay.
* **A-4 second exchange uses distinct budgets and spends part of the first:** `deadlines.uciok`
  and `deadlines.readyok` are set to different values (e.g. 100 ms and 300 ms); the test IO
  answers `uciok` only after ~70 % of the `uciok` budget, then delays the `isready` write by
  ~60 % of the `readyok` budget and floods non-`readyok` lines. Record `t_uciok` (paused-clock
  time at which `uciok` was read — e.g. observable from the test IO) and assert
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` with
  `readyok - read_interval <= elapsed - t_uciok <= readyok` — reusing the first exchange's
  deadline (fires ~30 ms after `uciok`) or starting after the `isready` write both fail.
* **Write-budget exhaustion:** for `stop` (A-1) and `uci` (A-4 first exchange), a test sets
  `deadlines.readyok` larger than the exchange budget, delays that command's write beyond the
  exchange budget but below `deadlines.readyok`, and asserts
  `EngineTimeout("writing engine command")` with `elapsed <= budget` from entry; the `stop` case
  also asserts runtime state `Terminating`. A call site that kept `send`'s separate
  `deadlines.readyok` write timeout (while the drain used the entry deadline) would overrun and
  fail. For the two `isready` exchanges the separate write bound *is* `deadlines.readyok`, the
  same value as the exchange budget, so an over-budget write fires at the same instant either
  way and there is no observable difference to test; their observable contract (whole exchange
  `<= deadlines.readyok` from entry) is proven by the delayed-write (60 %) cases' upper bound.
* **Cancellation unchanged (A-4):** a test cancels the token while `init_uci_cancellable` waits
  on a silent engine and asserts exactly `Err(Error::EngineDisconnected)` (the existing
  `terminate_all_reaps_an_actor_awaiting_uciok` only asserts `is_err()`).
* **Exact-token predicate preserved:** the existing
  `uci_acknowledgements_are_exact_and_timeout_is_bounded` asserts only `EngineDisconnected`
  after `uciok-not-an-ack` then EOF, which a helper accepting the prefix would also produce
  (it would write `isready`, then hit EOF). Extend it to assert that no `isready` was written.
* One actor-level test for A-2: `start_search` then `stop_current` on the actor with the chatty
  IO → `EngineTimeout`, `terminate_calls ≥ 1`, next `start_search` → `EngineDisconnected`.
* Budgets in tests are short (tens to low hundreds of ms of paused time).
* **Paused time needs Tokio's `test-util` feature**, which `full` does not include
  (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml` has `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["full"] }` and no Tokio
  dev-dependency; no test in the crate uses `start_paused` or `time::pause` today). Add
  `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["test-util"] }` under `[dev-dependencies]`.
  Measured 2026-09-27 on a copy of `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` + `Cargo.lock` (probe in
  `$RUN_TMP/probe-tokio`): `cargo metadata --locked --offline` exits 0, `Cargo.lock` is
  byte-identical (`cmp` equal), and the resolved `tokio@1.50.0` node gains `test-util`. So no
  lockfile change and no new crate.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Wall-clock budget, not stall detector plus separate cap, not N-lines-then-poison.** The
  finding listed three shapes. A separate cap on top of a per-line detector adds a second knob
  with no case where it helps: the per-line bound is subsumed by the wall-clock bound (any stall
  also exhausts the wall clock). A line count is not a time bound (a slow chatty engine still
  holds shutdown) and depends on engine verbosity. Rejected both.
* **Budget starts at entry to `stop_current`, including the `Stopping` re-entry branch.** Storing
  the original `stop` instant in `EngineState::Stopping` would change a `Copy + Eq` enum compared
  by value in `service_search_read` (`runtime.state != (EngineState::Stopping { request_id: id })`).
  The re-entry branch is reachable only if a previous `stop_current` future was dropped mid-drain;
  every production caller awaits it to completion inside the actor (`stop_at_protocol_boundary`,
  `start_search`). Worst case is therefore one extra budget, still bounded. Rejected: threading an
  `Instant` through the state.
* **`collect_engine_configuration` is not changed.** Its caller-side
  `tokio::time::timeout(EngineDeadlines::default().uciok, …)` wraps the whole configuration
  drain (`src-tauri/src/chess.rs`), so chatty lines cannot reset it — the mandate's defect is
  absent there. Its `uci` write, issued before that timeout starts, is separately bounded by
  `send`'s `deadlines.readyok`, so the probe is bounded by the sum. Plan-r2 wrongly claimed it
  already met O-1's write-inclusive exchange semantic (R2-04); plan-r3 then moved the write
  inside the timeout (O-5), which five lenses judged a behaviour change the mandate does not
  require (R3-01). The claim is corrected here and the probe left as is.
* **Why an exchange deadline includes its command write (not "from after sending").**
  The mandate's harm is `terminate_all` exceeding `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs`,
  15 s) with the child unreaped. Source trace (corrected in plan-r5; plan-r4 wrongly had
  `terminate_exact` calling `stop_current`): `terminate_all → terminate_targets →
  terminate_exact → EngineActor::terminate`, which cancels `interrupt` and sends
  `EngineCommand::Terminate` on the control channel (`process.rs` `EngineActor::terminate`).
  `engine_actor_loop` services one command at a time: while it is inside
  `stop_at_protocol_boundary → runtime.stop_current()` (a `Stop` from game-request cancellation
  via `wait_bestmove_cancellable` / `next_search_line_cancellable`) or inside `ensure_ready`, the
  queued `Terminate` is not read until that exchange returns, and neither `stop_current` nor
  `wait_for` observes `interrupt`. (`stop_generation` is a different shape with the same
  effect: it holds the key's lifecycle lease across `stop_current` then `terminate`, so a
  concurrent `terminate_exact` waits on that lease for the whole stop. `init_uci_cancellable`'s
  reads observe `interrupt`, but its `uci`/`isready` writes do not, so a blocked init write also
  delays a queued `Terminate`; under this plan that write is bounded by its exchange deadline,
  `uciok`/`readyok` 10 s — the same magnitude as today's `send` bound, so init's worst case is
  unchanged. Making writes observe `interrupt` is the rejected preemption alternative below.) The stop or readiness exchange's whole
  duration is therefore added in front of `terminate`'s `quit` 3 s + `kill_reap` 2 s. Stop,
  entry-based: 5 + 3 + 2 = 10 s ≤ 15 s; stop, write separately bounded by `deadlines.readyok`:
  10 + 5 + 3 + 2 = 20 s > 15 s — the mandate's harm survives. Same shape for `ensure_ready`
  (10 + 3 + 2 = 15 s vs 10 + 10 + 3 + 2 = 25 s). A write that cannot complete within the budget
  means the engine is not reading stdin (backpressured pipe) and has not received the command;
  poisoning it is the existing failed-write behaviour, reached sooner. Rejected alternative: keep `send`'s separate
  bound and bound only the drain (smaller diff, review-minimalism r4) — it leaves the shutdown
  overrun above. Not adopted either: making the in-flight exchange observe `interrupt` so
  `Terminate` preempts it — a change to the actor's command-serialization contract that the
  mandate (a deadline that never fires) does not require.
* **Cancellation precedence is not changed.** Making cancellation `biased` is not required by
  the mandate and has no evidenced failure; the helper keeps the existing unbiased select.
* **Defaults unchanged** (`stop` 5 s, `readyok`/`uciok` 10 s). Under the new semantic they are
  hard ceilings; no evidence that a conforming engine needs more.

## Risks / open questions

* A conforming engine under heavy load that takes > 5 s to emit `bestmove` after `stop` was
  already killed by the silent path; only a chatty one changes behaviour. That is the fix.
* `timeout_at` shares the tokio clock with `timeout`; existing tests using real time stay valid.

## Not part of this task

* `service_search_read`'s per-line `deadlines.search` (correct as a stall detector; O-1).
* `next_configuration_line_cancellable` per-line `uciok` bound and `collect_engine_configuration`
  (the command-side wall clock covers the configuration drain; see Decisions).
* `pnpm verify:app`: it cannot register an engine (native picker, `CLAUDE.md` "Verifying UI
  changes"), so it cannot drive a chatty engine; the changed logic is `EngineRuntime`'s, above
  the unchanged `ChildUciIo` read/write, and is proven by the runtime and actor tests.
* `f-20260911-02` (unscoped stop generation), `f-20260831-20` (reap after force-kill),
  `f-20260915-01`.
* `terminate()` / `terminate_child` budgets and `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` — unchanged; this fix makes
  `stop_current` finish inside its own budget so they are reached.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — wall-clock exchange helper, callers, tests, docs (one phase)

* Files: `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` (helper, `send` deadline form, `stop_current`,
  `ensure_ready`, `init_uci_cancellable`, `wait_for`, `wait_for_cancellable`, test IO, tests),
  `src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs` (doc comment), `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` (DO bullet),
  `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (Tokio `test-util` dev-dependency feature; `Cargo.lock` must stay
  unchanged — verify with `git diff --exit-code src-tauri/Cargo.lock`).
* Touches concurrency / process lifecycle (sensitive glob `src-tauri/src/engine/**`): no API
  contract, persistence or auth change; no Specta/binding change.
* Role: `sensitive`.
* Proof:
  `PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH" cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process`
  plus `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml -- --check` and
  `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`;
  final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md` "Rust/Tauri backend" (including
  `pnpm rust:windows:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure backend-test` and `backend-coverage`) and
  `pnpm gates:contract:check`; because `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` changes, also every gate whose
  toolchain it can affect, as that skill's "CI, dependencies, and release mechanics" section
  requires.

## Reviews

### Round 1 — revision plan-r1 (11 lenses, all plan-capable user-wide and project-wide)

Raw verdicts: review-plan REVISE · review-correctness REVISE · review-engine-protocol REVISE ·
review-tests REVISE · review-error-handling APPROVED (2 should-fix) · review-minimalism APPROVED ·
review-chess-semantics APPROVED · review-ipc-contract APPROVED · review-persisted-state APPROVED ·
review-pgn-index APPROVED · review-tauri-security APPROVED. Raw reports:
`$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r1.txt`.

* **R1-01** — the exchange deadline was created inside the read helper, after `stop_current`
  awaits `send("stop")` (bounded separately by `deadlines.readyok`), so a backpressured write
  plus a fresh drain budget exceeds A-1's bound from entry.
  Witnesses: review-plan r1 #1, review-correctness r1 #1, review-engine-protocol r1 #1,
  review-error-handling r1 #1 (same defect, framed as an undefined start point).
  Obligation: O-2, A-1, A-3. Disposition: **Fix** — the caller computes one absolute deadline at
  exchange entry, before the write; the write (`send` deadline form) and the drain share it;

  `init_uci_cancellable` exchanges. Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure
  check by review-plan, review-correctness, review-engine-protocol, review-error-handling.
* **R1-02** — A-4 claimed cancellation "wins immediately", but `wait_for_cancellable` uses an

  review-error-handling.
* **R1-03** — the outer test timeout "several times the budget" does not prove A-1's bound: a
  helper that scaled the budget or started after a delayed `stop` write would still pass.

### plan-r8.md

# Plan: bound every UCI protocol exchange by one wall-clock deadline (f-20260911-03)

## Goal

MANDATE (fixed, from `f-20260911-03`): an engine that keeps emitting output after `stop` — or
before `readyok` / `uciok` — must not keep the exchange alive past its deadline. Today
`EngineRuntime::stop_current` wraps each `read_line()` in `timeout(self.deadlines.stop, …)`
inside the drain loop, so every `info` line restarts the 5 s budget; `stop_current` never fails,
never poisons, and `terminate_all` can hit `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` with the child unreaped. `wait_for`
(and its twin `wait_for_cancellable`) reset per line the same way.

Acceptance:

* A-1 `stop_current` against an engine that emits `info` indefinitely and never `bestmove`
  returns `Error::EngineTimeout(_)` no later than `deadlines.stop` after it was entered — the
  `stop` write included — and leaves the runtime in `EngineState::Terminating`. The label is
  `"waiting for bestmove after stop"` when the budget expires during the drain and
  `"writing engine command"` when it expires during the `stop` write.
* A-2 Through the actor, that failure reaches `recover_failed_protocol`: the child is terminated
  (`UciIo::terminate` called) and the actor refuses further searches — the existing contract of
  `start_search_stop_timeout_reaps_the_actor_instead_of_accepting_another_go`, now also for the
  chatty engine.
* A-3 `ensure_ready` against an engine that emits non-`readyok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than `deadlines.readyok` after entry, the
  `isready` write included.
* A-4 `init_uci_cancellable` against an engine that emits non-`uciok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for uciok")` no later than `deadlines.uciok` after entry, the `uci`
  write included; against an engine that answers `uciok` and then emits non-`readyok` lines
  indefinitely it returns `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than
  `deadlines.readyok` after the second exchange's entry, the `isready` write included.
  Cancellation behaviour is unchanged: an observed cancellation still returns
  `Error::EngineDisconnected`, through the same (unbiased) `select!` as today — this plan makes
  no new precedence claim.
* A-7 A `Terminate` requested while the actor is inside a stop or readiness exchange (a `Stop` or
  `EnsureReady` command against an engine that floods `info` / non-`readyok` lines, or whose
  command write is blocked) is serviced promptly: `EngineActor::terminate` returns within a small
  bound (paused time: far below `deadlines.stop` / `deadlines.readyok`), the in-flight request
  returns an error, and the child is terminated (O-6).
* A-5 A well-behaved engine is unaffected: every existing engine test passes unchanged, including
  the exact timeout messages asserted at `process.rs` (`"waiting for bestmove after stop"`).
* A-6 Each **chatty-exchange** test (A-1 drain, A-3, both A-4 exchanges) goes red if the
  read-until helper is reverted to a per-line `timeout` (it then never completes and the test's
  outer bound fails it), if the deadline is started after the command write, if the budget is
  scaled beyond its configured value, or if it times out early (each asserts elapsed time from
  exchange entry against both an upper and a lower bound, with its command write delayed).
  Reuse of an earlier exchange's deadline is claimed only for A-4's second exchange, the one
  test whose prior exchange first consumes part of its budget. The write-exhaustion and cancellation tests prove
  their own named claims only.

## Approach

### O-1 Semantics of the deadlines (decides the finding's open question)

`deadlines.stop`, `deadlines.readyok` and `deadlines.uciok` are **wall-clock budgets for one
protocol exchange**, measured from entry into the waiting routine (for `stop_current`: before
`stop` is written), not per-line stall detectors. Reasoning: UCI requires the engine to answer
`stop` with `bestmove` "as soon as possible" and `isready` with `readyok` "as soon as possible";
neither exchange has a legitimate reason to run indefinitely while output keeps arriving. A
slow-but-alive engine that cannot answer `stop` within 5 s is already treated as broken by the
existing silent-timeout path; talking does not make it less broken. The per-line stall semantic
stays exactly where the protocol legitimately runs indefinitely: `deadlines.search` in
`service_search_read` (an infinite `go` streams `info` forever by design) — unchanged.

### O-2 One bounded read-until helper on `EngineRuntime` (rule 11: the three loops are one concept)

`stop_current`'s drain loop, `wait_for` and `wait_for_cancellable` are three copies of "read
lines until a terminal line, fail with `EngineTimeout(<label>)` on budget, `EngineDisconnected`
on EOF". An **exchange** is one command write plus the drain to its terminal line; its deadline
covers both. Replace the three loops with one private helper on `EngineRuntime` that:

* receives one absolute deadline (`tokio::time::Instant`) computed by the caller **once, at
  exchange entry, before the command is written**, and never recomputes it;
* bounds each `read_line()` with `tokio::time::timeout_at(deadline, …)`, so the remaining budget
  shrinks with every line;
* takes the terminal-line predicate (exact trimmed token for `uciok`/`readyok`, parsed
  `UciMessage::BestMove` for stop), the timeout label, and an optional `CancellationToken`
  (cancellation keeps its current precedence and error: `EngineDisconnected`);
* keeps `read_line()`'s existing per-line behaviour (logging, `MAX_ENGINE_LINE_BYTES`).

The command write of an exchange is bounded by the **same** deadline: `send` gains a
deadline-taking form (`timeout_at(deadline, io.write_line(…))`, label unchanged
`"writing engine command"`), and the existing `send(command)` becomes that form with
`now + deadlines.readyok`, so non-exchange writes keep today's bound and there is one write path.
Callers:

* `stop_current`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.stop`, computed before the state transition and
  before `stop` is written; the `stop` write and the drain share it. It keeps its state
  transitions (`Searching → Stopping`, then `Idle` on success / `Terminating` on any error,
  write timeout included). The `Stopping { .. }` re-entry branch (no second `stop` written) gets
  a fresh full budget from entry; see Decisions.
* `ensure_ready`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.readyok`, shared by the `isready` write and the
  drain.
* `init_uci_cancellable`: two exchanges — `uci`/`uciok` with entry + `deadlines.uciok`, then
  `isready`/`readyok` with a fresh entry + `deadlines.readyok` computed when that second
  exchange starts.

`wait_for` / `wait_for_cancellable` either become thin callers of the helper or disappear in
favour of it.

Error strings stay byte-identical: `"waiting for bestmove after stop"`, `"waiting for uciok"`,
`"waiting for readyok"`.

### O-6 Termination preempts an in-flight exchange

The actor already owns a termination signal: `EngineActor::terminate` cancels `interrupt`
before queuing `EngineCommand::Terminate`, and `init_uci_cancellable` passes that token to its
reads. Extend the same token to every runtime exchange the actor loop services — `stop_current`
(from `EngineCommand::Stop`, from `service_search_read`'s Stop arms, and from `start_search`'s
implicit stop) and `ensure_ready` (from `EngineCommand::EnsureReady` and `start_search`'s
resynchronisation) — for **both the command write and every read**: the O-2 helper and the
`send` deadline form take the optional token and return `Error::EngineDisconnected` when it is
cancelled (the existing `wait_for_cancellable` result). `init_uci_cancellable`'s writes get the
same treatment. Commands stay serialized; nothing new runs concurrently — an exchange that is
already running simply stops waiting once termination has been requested.

Failure semantics: an interrupted `stop_current` is a failed stop (`Terminating`, same as a
timeout), so `stop_at_protocol_boundary → recover_failed_protocol` terminates the runtime and
the actor loop exits; the queued `Terminate`'s `request_control` then gets
`EngineDisconnected`, which `EngineActor::terminate` already treats as success once the reap
task has finished. An interrupted `ensure_ready` replies with the error and the loop proceeds to
the queued `Terminate`.

Why required (MANDATE harm): without it, a `Terminate` queued behind a chatty `ensure_ready`
waits the full `deadlines.readyok` (10 s), then `terminate` spends `quit` 3 s + `kill_reap` 2 s +
`STDERR_REAP_TIMEOUT` 200 ms = 15.2 s > `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` 15 s (`src-tauri/src/main.rs`), so
`terminate_all` can still hit the shutdown budget with the child unreaped (R7-01). With O-6 the
queued-termination path costs ≈ 5.2 s regardless of the exchange in flight.

### O-3 Document the invariant where it is written

* `EngineDeadlines` doc comment (`src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs`): state that inside
  `EngineRuntime` `uciok`, `readyok` and `stop` are wall-clock budgets per exchange (command
  write included) and `search` is a per-line stall bound; and that `collect_engine_configuration`
  separately applies `uciok` as a caller-side wall clock over the configuration drain only
  (its `uci` write keeps `send`'s own bound).
* `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md`: one DO bullet — bound every wait for a terminal UCI
  line (`bestmove` after `stop`, `readyok`, `uciok`) by one absolute deadline that output cannot
  reset, never by a per-line timeout; inside `EngineRuntime` (`stop_current`, `ensure_ready`,
  `init_uci_cancellable`) that deadline is computed **before the command is written** and shared
  by the write and every read; and every exchange the actor loop services observes the actor's
  `interrupt` token on its write and reads, so a requested termination never waits behind it
  (O-6). The write-inclusive deadline still matters where termination is not signalled through
  `interrupt`: `stop_generation` holds the key's lifecycle lease across `stop_current` then
  `terminate`, so a concurrent `terminate_exact` waits for that whole stop. Name `f-20260911-03` as the incident. (The configuration
  probe, a caller-side wall clock over separate `ConfigureNext` requests, satisfies the first
  clause and is outside the second.)
* `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` is not edited: its comment in `collect_engine_configuration`
  ("the per-line timeout in `next_configuration_line` only protects a silent process") stays
  accurate, because `next_configuration_line_cancellable` is unchanged.

### O-4 Tests (proof of A-1…A-7)

In `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` `mod tests`:

* A test `UciIo` that returns an `info depth 1 …` line after a short sleep on every read,
  forever, never EOF (a small dedicated struct beside `GameCleanupTestIo`/`FakeIo`, or an
  "endless line" option on `FakeIo` — executor's choice; `FakeIo`'s queue is finite and its
  `read_delay` is one-shot, so it cannot express this today). Each read's sleep must be well
  below the budget so a per-line timeout would never fire.
* Runtime-level tests for A-1 (state is `Terminating` afterwards), A-3, A-4 (timeout; plus the
  existing cancellation path still returning `EngineDisconnected` when the token is cancelled
  while the engine is silent), each wrapped in an outer `tokio::time::timeout` several times
  the budget so a per-line regression fails instead of hanging.
* **Deadline precision (A-6):** these tests run under `#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]`, so
  tokio's clock advances deterministically, and assert `elapsed` measured with
  `tokio::time::Instant` from just before the call: `elapsed <= budget` (paused time makes this
  exact; allow at most one read interval of slack only if the helper's structure requires it,
  and state why). Each chatty-exchange test also asserts a **lower bound**
  `elapsed >= budget - read_interval`, so a deadline that fires early (e.g. a reused earlier
  deadline) fails. **Every exchange test delays its own command write** by a substantial
  fraction of that exchange's budget (e.g. 60 %) and still asserts both bounds from entry — the
  case a deadline started after the write fails: `stop` for A-1, `isready` for A-3, `uci` for
  A-4's first exchange. The test IO therefore needs a per-command write delay (keyed by the
  command text), not only a read delay.
* **A-4 second exchange uses distinct budgets and spends part of the first:** `deadlines.uciok`
  and `deadlines.readyok` are set to different values (e.g. 100 ms and 300 ms); the test IO
  answers `uciok` only after ~70 % of the `uciok` budget, then delays the `isready` write by
  ~60 % of the `readyok` budget and floods non-`readyok` lines. Record `t_uciok` (paused-clock
  time at which `uciok` was read — e.g. observable from the test IO) and assert
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` with
  `readyok - read_interval <= elapsed - t_uciok <= readyok` — reusing the first exchange's
  deadline (fires ~30 ms after `uciok`) or starting after the `isready` write both fail.
* **Write-budget exhaustion:** for `stop` (A-1) and `uci` (A-4 first exchange), a test sets
  `deadlines.readyok` larger than the exchange budget, delays that command's write beyond the
  exchange budget but below `deadlines.readyok`, and asserts
  `EngineTimeout("writing engine command")` with `elapsed <= budget` from entry; the `stop` case
  also asserts runtime state `Terminating`. A call site that kept `send`'s separate
  `deadlines.readyok` write timeout (while the drain used the entry deadline) would overrun and
  fail. For the two `isready` exchanges the separate write bound *is* `deadlines.readyok`, the
  same value as the exchange budget, so an over-budget write fires at the same instant either
  way and there is no observable difference to test; their observable contract (whole exchange
  `<= deadlines.readyok` from entry) is proven by the delayed-write (60 %) cases' upper bound.
* **Cancellation unchanged (A-4):** tests cancel the token while `init_uci_cancellable` waits
  on a silent engine — once during the `uci`/`uciok` exchange and once after `uciok` has been
  read, while `readyok` is pending — and assert exactly `Err(Error::EngineDisconnected)` (the
  existing `terminate_all_reaps_an_actor_awaiting_uciok` only asserts `is_err()`).
* **Termination preempts (A-7, O-6):** actor-level tests under paused time with default
  deadlines: (1) `start_search`, then a `stop_current` request against an IO that floods `info`
  forever, then `terminate()` — `terminate()` completes in far less than `deadlines.stop`, the
  stop request returns an error, `terminate_calls >= 1`; (2) an `ensure_ready` request against
  an IO that floods non-`readyok` lines, then `terminate()` — completes in far less than
  `deadlines.readyok`, the ready request returns an error; (3) as (2) but with the `isready`
  write blocked (write delay beyond `deadlines.readyok`) — `terminate()` still completes
  promptly. Each goes red if the exchange ignores `interrupt` (terminate then waits the full
  deadline).
* **Exact-token predicate preserved:** the existing
  `uci_acknowledgements_are_exact_and_timeout_is_bounded` asserts only `EngineDisconnected`
  after `uciok-not-an-ack` then EOF, which a helper accepting the prefix would also produce
  (it would write `isready`, then hit EOF). Extend it to assert that no `isready` was written.
* One actor-level test for A-2: `start_search` then `stop_current` on the actor with the chatty
  IO → `EngineTimeout`, `terminate_calls ≥ 1`, next `start_search` → `EngineDisconnected`.
* Budgets in tests are short (tens to low hundreds of ms of paused time).
* **Paused time needs Tokio's `test-util` feature**, which `full` does not include
  (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml` has `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["full"] }` and no Tokio
  dev-dependency; no test in the crate uses `start_paused` or `time::pause` today). Add
  `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["test-util"] }` under `[dev-dependencies]`.
  Measured 2026-09-27 on a copy of `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` + `Cargo.lock` (probe in
  `$RUN_TMP/probe-tokio`): `cargo metadata --locked --offline` exits 0, `Cargo.lock` is
  byte-identical (`cmp` equal), and the resolved `tokio@1.50.0` node gains `test-util`. So no
  lockfile change and no new crate.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Wall-clock budget, not stall detector plus separate cap, not N-lines-then-poison.** The
  finding listed three shapes. A separate cap on top of a per-line detector adds a second knob
  with no case where it helps: the per-line bound is subsumed by the wall-clock bound (any stall
  also exhausts the wall clock). A line count is not a time bound (a slow chatty engine still
  holds shutdown) and depends on engine verbosity. Rejected both.
* **Budget starts at entry to `stop_current`, including the `Stopping` re-entry branch.** Storing
  the original `stop` instant in `EngineState::Stopping` would change a `Copy + Eq` enum compared
  by value in `service_search_read` (`runtime.state != (EngineState::Stopping { request_id: id })`).
  The re-entry branch is reachable only if a previous `stop_current` future was dropped mid-drain;
  every production caller awaits it to completion inside the actor (`stop_at_protocol_boundary`,
  `start_search`). Worst case is therefore one extra budget, still bounded. Rejected: threading an
  `Instant` through the state.
* **`collect_engine_configuration` is not changed.** Its caller-side
  `tokio::time::timeout(EngineDeadlines::default().uciok, …)` wraps the whole configuration
  drain (`src-tauri/src/chess.rs`), so chatty lines cannot reset it — the mandate's defect is
  absent there. Its `uci` write, issued before that timeout starts, is separately bounded by
  `send`'s `deadlines.readyok`, so the probe is bounded by the sum. Plan-r2 wrongly claimed it
  already met O-1's write-inclusive exchange semantic (R2-04); plan-r3 then moved the write
  inside the timeout (O-5), which five lenses judged a behaviour change the mandate does not
  require (R3-01). The claim is corrected here and the probe left as is.
* **Why an exchange deadline includes its command write (not "from after sending").**
  The mandate's harm is `terminate_all` exceeding `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs`,
  15 s) with the child unreaped. Source trace (corrected in plan-r5; plan-r4 wrongly had
  `terminate_exact` calling `stop_current`): `terminate_all → terminate_targets →
  terminate_exact → EngineActor::terminate`, which cancels `interrupt` and sends
  `EngineCommand::Terminate` on the control channel (`process.rs` `EngineActor::terminate`).
  `engine_actor_loop` services one command at a time: at BASE, while it is inside
  `stop_at_protocol_boundary → runtime.stop_current()` (a `Stop` from game-request cancellation
  via `wait_bestmove_cancellable` / `next_search_line_cancellable`) or inside `ensure_ready`, the
  queued `Terminate` is not read until that exchange returns, and neither `stop_current` nor
  `wait_for` observes `interrupt` — O-6 removes that path. (`stop_generation` is a different shape with the same
  effect: it holds the key's lifecycle lease across `stop_current` then `terminate`, so a
  concurrent `terminate_exact` waits on that lease for the whole stop. `init_uci_cancellable`'s
  reads observe `interrupt`, but its `uci`/`isready` writes do not, so a blocked init write also
  delays a queued `Terminate`; under this plan that write is bounded by its exchange deadline,
  `uciok`/`readyok` 10 s — the same magnitude as today's `send` bound, so init's worst case is
  unchanged. Making writes observe `interrupt` is the rejected preemption alternative below.) The stop or readiness exchange's whole
  duration is therefore added in front of `terminate`'s `quit` 3 s + `kill_reap` 2 s. Stop,
  entry-based: 5 + 3 + 2 = 10 s ≤ 15 s; stop, write separately bounded by `deadlines.readyok`:
  10 + 5 + 3 + 2 = 20 s > 15 s — the mandate's harm survives. Same shape for `ensure_ready`
  (10 + 3 + 2 + 0.2 = 15.2 s vs 25.2 s — still over budget, hence O-6). After O-6 the
  queued-`Terminate` path no longer depends on the exchange deadline; the write-inclusive
  deadline remains what bounds the lease-held `stop_generation` path (stop 5 s + terminate 5.2 s
  = 10.2 s ≤ 15 s, versus 20.2 s with a separately bounded write) and ordinary latency. A write that cannot complete within the budget
  means the engine is not reading stdin (backpressured pipe) and has not received the command;
  poisoning it is the existing failed-write behaviour, reached sooner. Rejected alternative: keep `send`'s separate
  bound and bound only the drain (smaller diff, review-minimalism r4) — it leaves the shutdown
  lease-held `stop_generation` overrun above. **Reversal recorded (R7-01):** plan-r5…r7 rejected
  making the in-flight exchange observe `interrupt` as not required by the mandate; the r7
  evidence (readiness path 15.2 s > 15 s even with the exchange deadline) shows the mandate's
  stated harm survives without it, so O-6 adopts it. It changes no serialization: the token
  already exists, is already cancelled by `EngineActor::terminate`, and is already observed by
  `init_uci_cancellable`'s reads.
* **Cancellation precedence is not changed.** Making cancellation `biased` is not required by
  the mandate and has no evidenced failure; the helper keeps the existing unbiased select.
* **Defaults unchanged** (`stop` 5 s, `readyok`/`uciok` 10 s). Under the new semantic they are
  hard ceilings; no evidence that a conforming engine needs more.

## Risks / open questions

* A conforming engine under heavy load that takes > 5 s to emit `bestmove` after `stop` was
  already killed by the silent path; only a chatty one changes behaviour. That is the fix.
* `timeout_at` shares the tokio clock with `timeout`; existing tests using real time stay valid.

## Not part of this task

* `service_search_read`'s per-line `deadlines.search` (correct as a stall detector; O-1).
* `next_configuration_line_cancellable` per-line `uciok` bound and `collect_engine_configuration`
  (the command-side wall clock covers the configuration drain; see Decisions).
* `pnpm verify:app`: it cannot register an engine (native picker, `CLAUDE.md` "Verifying UI
  changes"), so it cannot drive a chatty engine; the changed logic is `EngineRuntime`'s, above
  the unchanged `ChildUciIo` read/write, and is proven by the runtime and actor tests.
* `f-20260911-02` (unscoped stop generation), `f-20260831-20` (reap after force-kill),
  `f-20260915-01`.
* `terminate()` / `terminate_child` budgets and `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` — unchanged; this fix makes
  `stop_current` finish inside its own budget so they are reached.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — wall-clock exchange helper, callers, tests, docs (one phase)

* Files: `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` (helper, `send` deadline form, `stop_current`,
  `ensure_ready`, `init_uci_cancellable`, `wait_for`, `wait_for_cancellable`,
  `stop_at_protocol_boundary` / `engine_actor_loop` / `service_search_read` token plumbing (O-6),
  test IO, tests),
  `src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs` (doc comment), `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` (DO bullet),
  `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (Tokio `test-util` dev-dependency feature; `Cargo.lock` must stay
  unchanged — verify with `git diff --exit-code src-tauri/Cargo.lock`).
* Touches concurrency / process lifecycle (sensitive glob `src-tauri/src/engine/**`): no API
  contract, persistence or auth change; no Specta/binding change.
* Role: `sensitive`.
* Proof:
  `PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH" cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process`
  plus `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml -- --check` and
  `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`;
  final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md` "Rust/Tauri backend" (including
  `pnpm rust:windows:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure backend-test` and `backend-coverage`) and
  `pnpm gates:contract:check`; because `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` changes, also every gate whose
  toolchain it can affect, as that skill's "CI, dependencies, and release mechanics" section
  requires.

### Round 1 — revision plan-r1 (11 lenses, all plan-capable user-wide and project-wide)

Raw verdicts: review-plan REVISE · review-correctness REVISE · review-engine-protocol REVISE ·
review-tests REVISE · review-error-handling APPROVED (2 should-fix) · review-minimalism APPROVED ·
review-chess-semantics APPROVED · review-ipc-contract APPROVED · review-persisted-state APPROVED ·
review-pgn-index APPROVED · review-tauri-security APPROVED. Raw reports:
`$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r1.txt`.

* **R1-01** — the exchange deadline was created inside the read helper, after `stop_current`
  awaits `send("stop")` (bounded separately by `deadlines.readyok`), so a backpressured write
  plus a fresh drain budget exceeds A-1's bound from entry.
  Witnesses: review-plan r1 #1, review-correctness r1 #1, review-engine-protocol r1 #1,
  review-error-handling r1 #1 (same defect, framed as an undefined start point).
  Obligation: O-2, A-1, A-3. Disposition: **Fix** — the caller computes one absolute deadline at
  exchange entry, before the write; the write (`send` deadline form) and the drain share it;
  A-1 names both timeout labels; same shape for `ensure_ready` and the two
  `init_uci_cancellable` exchanges. Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure
  check by review-plan, review-correctness, review-engine-protocol, review-error-handling.
* **R1-02** — A-4 claimed cancellation "wins immediately", but `wait_for_cancellable` uses an
  unbiased `tokio::select!`. Witness: review-error-handling r1 #2. Obligation: A-4.
  Disposition: **Fix** by narrowing — A-4 now claims unchanged cancellation behaviour only;
  Decisions records that precedence is not changed (not required by MANDATE, no evidenced
  failure). Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure check by review-plan,
  review-error-handling.
* **R1-03** — the outer test timeout "several times the budget" does not prove A-1's bound: a
  helper that scaled the budget or started after a delayed `stop` write would still pass.
  Witness: review-tests r1 #1. Obligation: O-4, A-6. Disposition: **Fix** — tests run under
  paused tokio time and assert `elapsed <= budget` from entry; the stop test delays the `stop`
  write by ~60 % of the budget. Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure
  check by review-plan, review-tests.

Adopted this round: 3 (R1-01, R1-02, R1-03).

### plan-r9.md

# Plan: bound every UCI protocol exchange by one wall-clock deadline (f-20260911-03)

## Goal

MANDATE (fixed, from `f-20260911-03`): an engine that keeps emitting output after `stop` — or
before `readyok` / `uciok` — must not keep the exchange alive past its deadline. Today
`EngineRuntime::stop_current` wraps each `read_line()` in `timeout(self.deadlines.stop, …)`
inside the drain loop, so every `info` line restarts the 5 s budget; `stop_current` never fails,
never poisons, and `terminate_all` can hit `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` with the child unreaped. `wait_for`
(and its twin `wait_for_cancellable`) reset per line the same way.

Acceptance:

* A-1 `stop_current` against an engine that emits `info` indefinitely and never `bestmove`
  returns `Error::EngineTimeout(_)` no later than `deadlines.stop` after it was entered — the
  `stop` write included — and leaves the runtime in `EngineState::Terminating`. The label is
  `"waiting for bestmove after stop"` when the budget expires during the drain and
  `"writing engine command"` when it expires during the `stop` write.
* A-2 Through the actor, that failure reaches `recover_failed_protocol`: the child is terminated
  (`UciIo::terminate` called) and the actor refuses further searches — the existing contract of
  `start_search_stop_timeout_reaps_the_actor_instead_of_accepting_another_go`, now also for the
  chatty engine.
* A-3 `ensure_ready` against an engine that emits non-`readyok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than `deadlines.readyok` after entry, the
  `isready` write included.
* A-4 `init_uci_cancellable` against an engine that emits non-`uciok` lines indefinitely returns
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for uciok")` no later than `deadlines.uciok` after entry, the `uci`
  write included; against an engine that answers `uciok` and then emits non-`readyok` lines
  indefinitely it returns `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` no later than
  `deadlines.readyok` after the second exchange's entry, the `isready` write included.
  Cancellation behaviour is unchanged: an observed cancellation still returns
  `Error::EngineDisconnected`, through the same (unbiased) `select!` as today — this plan makes
  no new precedence claim.
* A-5 A well-behaved engine is unaffected: every existing engine test passes unchanged, including
  the exact timeout messages asserted at `process.rs` (`"waiting for bestmove after stop"`).
* A-6 Each **chatty-exchange** test (A-1 drain, A-3, both A-4 exchanges) goes red if the
  read-until helper is reverted to a per-line `timeout` (it then never completes and the test's
  outer bound fails it), if the deadline is started after the command write, if the budget is
  scaled beyond its configured value, or if it times out early (each asserts elapsed time from
  exchange entry against both an upper and a lower bound, with its command write delayed).
  Reuse of an earlier exchange's deadline is claimed only for A-4's second exchange, the one
  test whose prior exchange first consumes part of its budget. The write-exhaustion and cancellation tests prove
  their own named claims only.

## Approach

### O-1 Semantics of the deadlines (decides the finding's open question)

`deadlines.stop`, `deadlines.readyok` and `deadlines.uciok` are **wall-clock budgets for one
protocol exchange**, measured from entry into the waiting routine (for `stop_current`: before
`stop` is written), not per-line stall detectors. Reasoning: UCI requires the engine to answer
`stop` with `bestmove` "as soon as possible" and `isready` with `readyok` "as soon as possible";
neither exchange has a legitimate reason to run indefinitely while output keeps arriving. A
slow-but-alive engine that cannot answer `stop` within 5 s is already treated as broken by the
existing silent-timeout path; talking does not make it less broken. The per-line stall semantic
stays exactly where the protocol legitimately runs indefinitely: `deadlines.search` in
`service_search_read` (an infinite `go` streams `info` forever by design) — unchanged.

### O-2 One bounded read-until helper on `EngineRuntime` (rule 11: the three loops are one concept)

`stop_current`'s drain loop, `wait_for` and `wait_for_cancellable` are three copies of "read
lines until a terminal line, fail with `EngineTimeout(<label>)` on budget, `EngineDisconnected`
on EOF". An **exchange** is one command write plus the drain to its terminal line; its deadline
covers both. Replace the three loops with one private helper on `EngineRuntime` that:

* receives one absolute deadline (`tokio::time::Instant`) computed by the caller **once, at
  exchange entry, before the command is written**, and never recomputes it;
* bounds each `read_line()` with `tokio::time::timeout_at(deadline, …)`, so the remaining budget
  shrinks with every line;
* takes the terminal-line predicate (exact trimmed token for `uciok`/`readyok`, parsed
  `UciMessage::BestMove` for stop), the timeout label, and an optional `CancellationToken`
  (cancellation keeps its current precedence and error: `EngineDisconnected`);
* keeps `read_line()`'s existing per-line behaviour (logging, `MAX_ENGINE_LINE_BYTES`).

The command write of an exchange is bounded by the **same** deadline: `send` gains a
deadline-taking form (`timeout_at(deadline, io.write_line(…))`, label unchanged
`"writing engine command"`), and the existing `send(command)` becomes that form with
`now + deadlines.readyok`, so non-exchange writes keep today's bound and there is one write path.
Callers:

* `stop_current`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.stop`, computed before the state transition and
  before `stop` is written; the `stop` write and the drain share it. It keeps its state
  transitions (`Searching → Stopping`, then `Idle` on success / `Terminating` on any error,
  write timeout included). The `Stopping { .. }` re-entry branch (no second `stop` written) gets
  a fresh full budget from entry; see Decisions.
* `ensure_ready`: deadline = entry + `deadlines.readyok`, shared by the `isready` write and the
  drain.
* `init_uci_cancellable`: two exchanges — `uci`/`uciok` with entry + `deadlines.uciok`, then
  `isready`/`readyok` with a fresh entry + `deadlines.readyok` computed when that second
  exchange starts.

`wait_for` / `wait_for_cancellable` either become thin callers of the helper or disappear in
favour of it.

Error strings stay byte-identical: `"waiting for bestmove after stop"`, `"waiting for uciok"`,
`"waiting for readyok"`.

### O-3 Document the invariant where it is written

* `EngineDeadlines` doc comment (`src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs`): state that inside
  `EngineRuntime` `uciok`, `readyok` and `stop` are wall-clock budgets per exchange (command
  write included) and `search` is a per-line stall bound; and that `collect_engine_configuration`
  separately applies `uciok` as a caller-side wall clock over the configuration drain only
  (its `uci` write keeps `send`'s own bound).
* `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md`: one DO bullet — bound every wait for a terminal UCI
  line (`bestmove` after `stop`, `readyok`, `uciok`) by one absolute deadline that output cannot
  reset, never by a per-line timeout; inside `EngineRuntime` (`stop_current`, `ensure_ready`,
  `init_uci_cancellable`) that deadline is computed **before the command is written** and shared
  by the write and every read, because termination can wait behind a whole stop or readiness
  exchange (the actor serializes commands and these exchanges do not observe `interrupt`;
  `stop_generation` holds the lifecycle lease), so a second, separate write budget pushes
  shutdown further past `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET`. Only the reads of `init_uci_cancellable` observe
  `interrupt`; its writes are bounded by the same deadline. Name `f-20260911-03` as the incident. (The configuration
  probe, a caller-side wall clock over separate `ConfigureNext` requests, satisfies the first
  clause and is outside the second.)
* `src-tauri/src/chess.rs` is not edited: its comment in `collect_engine_configuration`
  ("the per-line timeout in `next_configuration_line` only protects a silent process") stays
  accurate, because `next_configuration_line_cancellable` is unchanged.

### O-4 Tests (proof of A-1…A-6)

In `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` `mod tests`:

* A test `UciIo` that returns an `info depth 1 …` line after a short sleep on every read,
  forever, never EOF (a small dedicated struct beside `GameCleanupTestIo`/`FakeIo`, or an
  "endless line" option on `FakeIo` — executor's choice; `FakeIo`'s queue is finite and its
  `read_delay` is one-shot, so it cannot express this today). Each read's sleep must be well
  below the budget so a per-line timeout would never fire.
* Runtime-level tests for A-1 (state is `Terminating` afterwards), A-3, A-4 (timeout; plus the
  existing cancellation path still returning `EngineDisconnected` when the token is cancelled
  while the engine is silent), each wrapped in an outer `tokio::time::timeout` several times
  the budget so a per-line regression fails instead of hanging.
* **Deadline precision (A-6):** these tests run under `#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]`, so
  tokio's clock advances deterministically, and assert `elapsed` measured with
  `tokio::time::Instant` from just before the call: `elapsed <= budget` (paused time makes this
  exact; allow at most one read interval of slack only if the helper's structure requires it,
  and state why). Each chatty-exchange test also asserts a **lower bound**
  `elapsed >= budget - read_interval`, so a deadline that fires early (e.g. a reused earlier
  deadline) fails. **Every exchange test delays its own command write** by a substantial
  fraction of that exchange's budget (e.g. 60 %) and still asserts both bounds from entry — the
  case a deadline started after the write fails: `stop` for A-1, `isready` for A-3, `uci` for
  A-4's first exchange. The test IO therefore needs a per-command write delay (keyed by the
  command text), not only a read delay.
* **A-4 second exchange uses distinct budgets and spends part of the first:** `deadlines.uciok`
  and `deadlines.readyok` are set to different values (e.g. 100 ms and 300 ms); the test IO
  answers `uciok` only after ~70 % of the `uciok` budget, then delays the `isready` write by
  ~60 % of the `readyok` budget and floods non-`readyok` lines. Record `t_uciok` (paused-clock
  time at which `uciok` was read — e.g. observable from the test IO) and assert
  `EngineTimeout("waiting for readyok")` with
  `readyok - read_interval <= elapsed - t_uciok <= readyok` — reusing the first exchange's
  deadline (fires ~30 ms after `uciok`) or starting after the `isready` write both fail.
* **Write-budget exhaustion:** for `stop` (A-1) and `uci` (A-4 first exchange), a test sets
  `deadlines.readyok` larger than the exchange budget, delays that command's write beyond the
  exchange budget but below `deadlines.readyok`, and asserts
  `EngineTimeout("writing engine command")` with `elapsed <= budget` from entry; the `stop` case
  also asserts runtime state `Terminating`. A call site that kept `send`'s separate
  `deadlines.readyok` write timeout (while the drain used the entry deadline) would overrun and
  fail. For the two `isready` exchanges the separate write bound *is* `deadlines.readyok`, the
  same value as the exchange budget, so an over-budget write fires at the same instant either
  way and there is no observable difference to test; their observable contract (whole exchange
  `<= deadlines.readyok` from entry) is proven by the delayed-write (60 %) cases' upper bound.
* **Cancellation unchanged (A-4):** tests cancel the token while `init_uci_cancellable` waits
  on a silent engine — once during the `uci`/`uciok` exchange and once after `uciok` has been
  read, while `readyok` is pending — and assert exactly `Err(Error::EngineDisconnected)` (the
  existing `terminate_all_reaps_an_actor_awaiting_uciok` only asserts `is_err()`).
* **Exact-token predicate preserved:** the existing
  `uci_acknowledgements_are_exact_and_timeout_is_bounded` asserts only `EngineDisconnected`
  after `uciok-not-an-ack` then EOF, which a helper accepting the prefix would also produce
  (it would write `isready`, then hit EOF). Extend it to assert that no `isready` was written.
* One actor-level test for A-2: `start_search` then `stop_current` on the actor with the chatty
  IO → `EngineTimeout`, `terminate_calls ≥ 1`, next `start_search` → `EngineDisconnected`.
* Budgets in tests are short (tens to low hundreds of ms of paused time).
* **Paused time needs Tokio's `test-util` feature**, which `full` does not include
  (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml` has `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["full"] }` and no Tokio
  dev-dependency; no test in the crate uses `start_paused` or `time::pause` today). Add
  `tokio = { version = "1.33", features = ["test-util"] }` under `[dev-dependencies]`.
  Measured 2026-09-27 on a copy of `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` + `Cargo.lock` (probe in
  `$RUN_TMP/probe-tokio`): `cargo metadata --locked --offline` exits 0, `Cargo.lock` is
  byte-identical (`cmp` equal), and the resolved `tokio@1.50.0` node gains `test-util`. So no
  lockfile change and no new crate.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Wall-clock budget, not stall detector plus separate cap, not N-lines-then-poison.** The
  finding listed three shapes. A separate cap on top of a per-line detector adds a second knob
  with no case where it helps: the per-line bound is subsumed by the wall-clock bound (any stall
  also exhausts the wall clock). A line count is not a time bound (a slow chatty engine still
  holds shutdown) and depends on engine verbosity. Rejected both.
* **Budget starts at entry to `stop_current`, including the `Stopping` re-entry branch.** Storing
  the original `stop` instant in `EngineState::Stopping` would change a `Copy + Eq` enum compared
  by value in `service_search_read` (`runtime.state != (EngineState::Stopping { request_id: id })`).
  The re-entry branch is reachable only if a previous `stop_current` future was dropped mid-drain;
  every production caller awaits it to completion inside the actor (`stop_at_protocol_boundary`,
  `start_search`). Worst case is therefore one extra budget, still bounded. Rejected: threading an
  `Instant` through the state.
* **`collect_engine_configuration` is not changed.** Its caller-side
  `tokio::time::timeout(EngineDeadlines::default().uciok, …)` wraps the whole configuration
  drain (`src-tauri/src/chess.rs`), so chatty lines cannot reset it — the mandate's defect is
  absent there. Its `uci` write, issued before that timeout starts, is separately bounded by
  `send`'s `deadlines.readyok`, so the probe is bounded by the sum. Plan-r2 wrongly claimed it
  already met O-1's write-inclusive exchange semantic (R2-04); plan-r3 then moved the write
  inside the timeout (O-5), which five lenses judged a behaviour change the mandate does not
  require (R3-01). The claim is corrected here and the probe left as is.
* **Why an exchange deadline includes its command write (not "from after sending").**
  The mandate's harm is `terminate_all` exceeding `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs`,
  15 s) with the child unreaped. Source trace (corrected in plan-r5; plan-r4 wrongly had
  `terminate_exact` calling `stop_current`): `terminate_all → terminate_targets →
  terminate_exact → EngineActor::terminate`, which cancels `interrupt` and sends
  `EngineCommand::Terminate` on the control channel (`process.rs` `EngineActor::terminate`).
  `engine_actor_loop` services one command at a time: at BASE, while it is inside
  `stop_at_protocol_boundary → runtime.stop_current()` (a `Stop` from game-request cancellation
  via `wait_bestmove_cancellable` / `next_search_line_cancellable`) or inside `ensure_ready`, the
  queued `Terminate` is not read until that exchange returns, and neither `stop_current` nor
  `wait_for` observes `interrupt` (this plan does not change that; see the deferred finding
  below). (`stop_generation` is a different shape with the same
  effect: it holds the key's lifecycle lease across `stop_current` then `terminate`, so a
  concurrent `terminate_exact` waits on that lease for the whole stop. `init_uci_cancellable`'s
  reads observe `interrupt`, but its `uci`/`isready` writes do not, so a blocked init write also
  delays a queued `Terminate`; under this plan that write is bounded by its exchange deadline,
  `uciok`/`readyok` 10 s — the same magnitude as today's `send` bound, so init's worst case is
  unchanged. Making writes observe `interrupt` is the deferred preemption question below.) The stop or readiness exchange's whole
  duration is therefore added in front of `terminate`'s `quit` 3 s + `kill_reap` 2 s. Stop,
  entry-based: 5 + 3 + 2 = 10 s ≤ 15 s; stop, write separately bounded by `deadlines.readyok`:
  10 + 5 + 3 + 2 = 20 s > 15 s — the mandate's harm survives. Same shape for `ensure_ready`
  (10 + 3 + 2 + 0.2 = 15.2 s vs 25.2 s; `STDERR_REAP_TIMEOUT` 200 ms included). The stop
  totals with that join are 10.2 s vs 20.2 s. The residual 15.2 s readiness case is **not** the
  mandate's defect: it is today's bound for a merely *silent* engine, where no per-line reset
  happens, so it is independent of this fix (R8-01). A write that cannot complete within the budget
  means the engine is not reading stdin (backpressured pipe) and has not received the command;
  poisoning it is the existing failed-write behaviour, reached sooner. Rejected alternative: keep `send`'s separate
  bound and bound only the drain (smaller diff, review-minimalism r4) — it leaves the shutdown
  stop overrun above. **Deferred, filed separately:** making every in-flight exchange observe
  the actor's `interrupt` token so a queued `Terminate` preempts it (or re-sizing the budgets)
  — the design question behind the residual silent-engine 15.2 s readiness overrun. plan-r8
  carried it as O-6; round 8 showed it exceeds the mandate (stop already fits: 10.2 s) and
  raises its own design questions (StartSearch vs EnsureReady failure paths, a successful
  `terminate` over an unconfirmed reap, preemption test ordering). Filed through the drain
  inbox as a `build`-tier `engine-uci` finding (R8-01).
* **Cancellation precedence is not changed.** Making cancellation `biased` is not required by
  the mandate and has no evidenced failure; the helper keeps the existing unbiased select.
* **Defaults unchanged** (`stop` 5 s, `readyok`/`uciok` 10 s). Under the new semantic they are
  hard ceilings; no evidence that a conforming engine needs more.

## Risks / open questions

* A conforming engine under heavy load that takes > 5 s to emit `bestmove` after `stop` was
  already killed by the silent path; only a chatty one changes behaviour. That is the fix.
* `timeout_at` shares the tokio clock with `timeout`; existing tests using real time stay valid.

## Not part of this task

* `service_search_read`'s per-line `deadlines.search` (correct as a stall detector; O-1).
* `next_configuration_line_cancellable` per-line `uciok` bound and `collect_engine_configuration`
  (the command-side wall clock covers the configuration drain; see Decisions).
* `pnpm verify:app`: it cannot register an engine (native picker, `CLAUDE.md` "Verifying UI
  changes"), so it cannot drive a chatty engine; the changed logic is `EngineRuntime`'s, above
  the unchanged `ChildUciIo` read/write, and is proven by the runtime and actor tests.
* `f-20260911-02` (unscoped stop generation), `f-20260831-20` (reap after force-kill),
  `f-20260915-01`.
* `terminate()` / `terminate_child` budgets and `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` — unchanged; this fix makes
  `stop_current` finish inside its own budget so they are reached.
* Termination preempting an in-flight exchange (`interrupt` on every runtime exchange) and the
  silent-engine readiness overrun — deferred to its own `build` finding (R8-01), filed through
  the drain inbox.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — wall-clock exchange helper, callers, tests, docs (one phase)

* Files: `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs` (helper, `send` deadline form, `stop_current`,
  `ensure_ready`, `init_uci_cancellable`, `wait_for`, `wait_for_cancellable`,
  test IO, tests),
  `src-tauri/src/engine/types.rs` (doc comment), `.claude/rules/engine-lifecycle.md` (DO bullet),
  `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (Tokio `test-util` dev-dependency feature; `Cargo.lock` must stay
  unchanged — verify with `git diff --exit-code src-tauri/Cargo.lock`).
* Touches concurrency / process lifecycle (sensitive glob `src-tauri/src/engine/**`): no API
  contract, persistence or auth change; no Specta/binding change.
* Role: `sensitive`.
* Proof:
  `PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH" cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked engine::process`
  plus `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml -- --check` and
  `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`;
  final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md` "Rust/Tauri backend" (including
  `pnpm rust:windows:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure backend-test` and `backend-coverage`) and
  `pnpm gates:contract:check`; because `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` changes, also every gate whose
  toolchain it can affect, as that skill's "CI, dependencies, and release mechanics" section
  requires.

## Reviews

### Round 1 — revision plan-r1 (11 lenses, all plan-capable user-wide and project-wide)

Raw verdicts: review-plan REVISE · review-correctness REVISE · review-engine-protocol REVISE ·
review-tests REVISE · review-error-handling APPROVED (2 should-fix) · review-minimalism APPROVED ·
review-chess-semantics APPROVED · review-ipc-contract APPROVED · review-persisted-state APPROVED ·
review-pgn-index APPROVED · review-tauri-security APPROVED. Raw reports:
`$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r1.txt`.

* **R1-01** — the exchange deadline was created inside the read helper, after `stop_current`
  awaits `send("stop")` (bounded separately by `deadlines.readyok`), so a backpressured write
  plus a fresh drain budget exceeds A-1's bound from entry.
  Witnesses: review-plan r1 #1, review-correctness r1 #1, review-engine-protocol r1 #1,
  review-error-handling r1 #1 (same defect, framed as an undefined start point).
  Obligation: O-2, A-1, A-3. Disposition: **Fix** — the caller computes one absolute deadline at
  exchange entry, before the write; the write (`send` deadline form) and the drain share it;
  A-1 names both timeout labels; same shape for `ensure_ready` and the two
  `init_uci_cancellable` exchanges. Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure
  check by review-plan, review-correctness, review-engine-protocol, review-error-handling.
* **R1-02** — A-4 claimed cancellation "wins immediately", but `wait_for_cancellable` uses an
  unbiased `tokio::select!`. Witness: review-error-handling r1 #2. Obligation: A-4.
  Disposition: **Fix** by narrowing — A-4 now claims unchanged cancellation behaviour only;
  Decisions records that precedence is not changed (not required by MANDATE, no evidenced
  failure). Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure check by review-plan,
  review-error-handling.
* **R1-03** — the outer test timeout "several times the budget" does not prove A-1's bound: a
  helper that scaled the budget or started after a delayed `stop` write would still pass.
  Witness: review-tests r1 #1. Obligation: O-4, A-6. Disposition: **Fix** — tests run under
  paused tokio time and assert `elapsed <= budget` from entry; the stop test delays the `stop`
  write by ~60 % of the budget. Correction revision: plan-r2. Status: open until r2 closure
  check by review-plan, review-tests.

Adopted this round: 3 (R1-01, R1-02, R1-03).

  source trace re-done by the orchestrator: `EngineActor::terminate` cancels `interrupt` and
  queues `Terminate` on the control channel; `engine_actor_loop` services one command at a time
  and `stop_current`/`wait_for` do not observe `interrupt`, so a `Terminate` queued behind an
  in-flight `Stop` (game cancellation or `stop_generation`) or `EnsureReady` waits for the whole
  exchange; arithmetic: stop 10 s entry-based vs 20 s with a separately bounded write, against
  `SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` 15 s. Decisions bullet rewritten with the corrected trace, the rejected
  drain-only alternative and the rejected interrupt-preemption alternative. Write-inclusive
  semantic kept. Focused fresh-context judgment requested from review-plan in r5 with this trace;
  review-minimalism re-runs as the dissenting witness. Correction revision: plan-r5.

  write timeout while the drain uses the entry deadline would pass the 60 % delayed-write tests.
  Witness: review-tests r4 #1. Obligation: O-4, A-1, A-4, A-6. Disposition: **Fix** —
  over-budget write tests for `stop` and `uci` with `deadlines.readyok` set larger than the
  exchange budget; for the `isready` exchanges the separate bound equals the budget, so the two
  implementations are observably identical and the 60 % upper-bound test states the contract.
